# z52b lane `upgrades` — almost-all → every upgrades in history, and what each could mean for Erdős–Straus

**ES is OPEN.** Nothing here proves or claims to prove it. This is a breadth-first list of candidate connections; the only filter
was "drop obvious nonsense" (false, meaningless, category error, literal duplicate). Items were **not** screened against the
barrier checklist (B1–B6 etc.), on purpose. Data: `data/z52b/upgrades.jsonl` (one JSON object per item); generator:
`scripts/z52b_upgrades_build.py` (catalogue `scripts/z52b_upgrades_catalogue.py`, items `scripts/z52b_upgrades_items*.py`,
this header `scripts/z52b_upgrades_header.md`). Regenerate with `python3 scripts/z52b_upgrades_build.py`.

## Summary

* **344 items** from **123 catalogued historical upgrades** (almost-all / density-one / infinitely-many / conditional /
  randomized / case-by-case → every or all-sufficiently-large), across additive and multiplicative number theory, quadratic forms,
  Diophantine equations, automorphic forms, homogeneous dynamics, additive combinatorics, extremal/probabilistic
  combinatorics, Egyptian fractions, function fields, model theory, complexity theory and topology. Also catalogued: **failed or
  stuck upgrades** (binary Goldbach, Apollonian local–global [false], Goldbach's other conjecture [false], Erdős's p + 2^k
  covering obstruction, Ghosh–Sarnak Markoff Hasse failures, Zaremba, Collatz, Lehmer τ(n) ≠ 0, Littlewood, p + m², three
  cubes) because they show where the step can fail.
* Confidence-not-nonsense: med 135, low 106, high 103; effort: cheap 193, medium 129, big 22. 553 distinct free-form tags; top 40: `numerics` 65, `odd` 25, `windows` 11, `levels` 11, `pigeonhole` 9, `parity` 9, `exceptional-set` 8, `egyptian-fractions` 8, `T1` 8, `identities` 7, `positivity` 7, `exponential-families` 7, `congruences` 7, `meta-strategy` 6, `BM-main-term` 6, `strengthening` 6, `sub-routes` 6, `verification` 5, `descent` 5, `e-slice` 5, `CRT` 5, `short-intervals` 5, `square-classes` 5, `entropy` 5, `induction` 5, `disproof-hygiene` 5, `literature` 5, `least-nonresidue` 4, `ineffective` 4, `circle-method` 4, `class-numbers` 4, `equidistribution` 4, `explicit` 4, `multiplicative-sets` 4, `profinite` 4, `B1` 4, `speculative` 4, `polynomial-method` 4, `zero-sum` 4, `closure-operations` 4.
* 109 items carry a `seen:<file>` tag (a quick keyword grep or my own
  knowledge of the repo — not an exhaustive novelty check).
* Every catalogue entry's "last step" was checked by research subagents (`data/z52b/upgrades_verify_out_{0..5}.md`); ~60 corrections
  were applied as overrides (two verifier "corrections" were rejected and say so: Dirichlet's Pell proof, Ramanujan's τ ≡ σ₁₁ (mod
  691)). Item-level literature leads were checked separately (`data/z52b/upgrades_leads_check.md`); unverified leads are phrased
  "check whether …".

### Template taxonomy (how upgrades actually happened) → ES status in one line each

1. **Explicit threshold + verification** (Helfgott, BDD g(4), Bertrand, BHV): ES has no all-large-p theorem at all, so the first
   gap is any effective statement (items 0001, 0002, 0217, 0222).
2. **Complementary methods / pick one of several** (Holowinsky–Soundararajan, Heath-Brown's Artin, Heilbronn's case split): the
   (★) and b=1 lines never fail together ≤ 10⁸ — the most concrete data-backed template (items 0095, 0096, 0059, 0061, 0284, 0327).
3. **Pigeonhole + group law** (Pell/Dirichlet, Lagrange, Spencer): needs a combination law on near-certificates; the SL₂ form is
   the candidate (items 0084, 0023, 0025, 0168, 0205).
4. **Local–global via one class / equidistribution of always-existing objects** (Gauss three squares, Duke, Linnik): needs an
   ES encoding with class number one or canonical objects attached to every p (items 0031, 0036, 0039, 0040, 0319).
5. **Congruence / divisibility forcing** (Chen's Markoff theorem, Ono, Lehmer's τ, Wielandt, Mihăilescu): find a nonzero
   congruence for a certificate count (items 0105, 0154, 0228, 0239, 0269).
6. **Parity / involution / topology** (Zagier, Kneser, Galois parity): finite sets with forced odd size or nontrivial ℤ/2-index
   (items 0026, 0027, 0099, 0155, 0257).
7. **Hecke/multiplicative rigidity** (QUE, Soundararajan): relations between counts at p, pℓ, pℓ² (items 0092, 0285).
8. **Amplification / complement counting** (Friedlander–Iwaniec, Stepanov–Weil): items 0097, 0090, 0331.
9. **Closure / recursion / absorption** (Wilson, Graham, Keevash, Bose–Shrikhande–Parker): ES is closed only under n → kn;
   items 0136, 0138, 0148, 0255.
10. **Exact formulas / identities outside Schinzel's reach** (Rademacher, Mordell's 3G(n) − 3, identities in (a, b) or m):
    items 0176, 0232, 0276, 0328.
11. **Characterise the exceptional set exactly** (Yoccoz/Brjuno, Apollonian corrected conjecture): items 0267, 0213, 0107.
12. **Failed upgrades as hygiene** (Apollonian, Ghosh–Sarnak, Erdős covering, 8/n, fake models): items 0108, 0322, 0323, 0171,
    0190, 0118.
13. **Self-corrections** found while generating: 0265 (power-size main terms are not sufficient: p + m² is open), 0275
    (Green–Tao–Ziegler is not uniform in coefficients at fixed p), 0315 (a single level already gives density one — the
    information is all in exceptional-set decay).

### Ten items I find most exciting (cheap first steps, and either new to the repo or sharper than what I found there)

1. **upgrades-0151** — ES as an infinite congruence "covering" of the non-squares (Hough / Balister et al. distortion method): the
   (★) congruences p ≡ −(u+B) (mod 4uB) never cover odd squares (T1); study the uncovered set as moduli grow.
2. **upgrades-0095 / 0096** — Holowinsky–Soundararajan template: find one statistic (e.g. L(1,(·/p)) or the BM correction T(p))
   such that (★) failure and b=1 failure push it in opposite directions.
3. **upgrades-0105** — Chen's Markoff mechanism: look for a congruence R(p)/2 ≡ g(p) ≢ 0 (mod m) on residue classes (cheap mining;
   cf. Lehmer's τ, items 0269–0270, and Ono-type mod-ℓ structure, 0154 and 0228).
4. **upgrades-0084** — Dirichlet's Pell proof (fails exactly at squares, like T1): pigeonhole two near-certificates in M₂(ℤ) with
   the same defect and take a quotient.
5. **upgrades-0092** — Soundararajan's no-escape-of-mass: Hecke-type relations between ES counts at p, pℓ, pℓ².
6. **upgrades-0090** — Stepanov/Weil "lower bound from upper bounds on the complements": the total over all divisor classes is
   known pointwise in p; only upper bounds on wrong classes are needed.
7. **upgrades-0328** — Mordell (1923): for prime n the number of positive solutions of yz + zx + xy = n is 3G(n) − 3, an exact
   class-number-sized count; look for maps from these to ES solutions.
8. **upgrades-0232 / 0276** — identity searches outside Mordell–Schinzel's scope: polynomial in the Gaussian coordinates (a, b)
   of p = a² + b², or in m for n = m² + k.
9. **upgrades-0201** — Linnik with a fixed split prime: prove ES for all p with (p/ℓ) = −1 for one fixed ℓ (BM layer guaranteed):
   the first possible infinite every-p family of Mordell-hard primes.
10. **upgrades-0314 + 0316** — positivity means there are no minor arcs: ES ⟸ a certificate at levels C ≤ (log p)^A, i.e. a sieve of
    growing dimension over the thin multiplicative sets S_C (failures of single levels have density 0 already at C = 1, item 0315).

Honourable mentions: 0191 (exact group-ring formula for window counts), 0097 (amplified short-family moment), 0241 (LPS → MSS:
arithmetic constructions stop at a class barrier, existence methods cross it), 0322 (why Ghosh–Sarnak's failure mechanism might
or might not apply to ES), 0222 (an every-n bound must save in log x, not log H).

## Method

1. Catalogued historical upgrades (brief's list + ~70 more), each with *before*, *after*, and the **last-step ingredient** — the
   thing that actually crossed from "almost all" to "every". 99 entries were checked by four research subagents, 24 later ones by
   two more; corrections are applied in the catalogue file as `FIX`/`FIX2` overrides and the raw reports kept in `data/z52b/`.
2. For each upgrade, generated items by asking: (a) can the last-step ingredient be **imported** literally (same tool on an ES
   count)? (b) **imitated** (same proof shape with ES objects)? (c) **analogised** (dictionary between the objects)? (d) what is the
   **intermediate statement** that would be ES's analogue of the historical intermediate step? (e) what does the upgrade's
   *failure* (if it failed) warn about? Several odd items are included on purpose (marked `odd`).
3. Eight generation passes; later passes deliberately revisited the strongest templates and filled upgrades with ≤ 1 item.
   Two self-corrections were added as items when verification showed a template does not transfer as stated
   (Green–Tao–Ziegler uniformity at fixed p: item 0275; power-size main terms are not sufficient, p + m² is open: item 0265).
4. `seen:` tags come from specific-keyword `git grep` over `*.md` (and my knowledge of the repo); they mean "related material
   exists there", not "this exact item was done".

## Coverage notes / what I did NOT get to

* Not done: any of the computations proposed (all first steps are untested); no deep reading of the primary sources beyond
  abstracts and the verifiers' checks; no systematic novelty check against the ~470 brainstorm memos beyond keyword greps.
* Under-covered fields: PDE/analysis "almost sure → deterministic" (only Kolmogorov/Carleson as a warning), mathematical physics
  beyond Lee–Yang, logic beyond Ax–Kochen, computational complexity beyond AKS/Reingold, algebraic topology beyond Kneser/Perelman.
* Not catalogued (considered, skipped as too far or redundant): Kepler, Hadamard matrices (open), Szemerédi/van der Waerden
  (colouring → density is the reverse direction), Hindman, Poincaré beyond one odd item, uniform Mordell.
* Several historical "before/after" summaries are compressed; for anything load-bearing, read the source named in the catalogue.

## Catalogue of historical upgrades (before → after; the last-step ingredient)

Verification status from `data/z52b/upgrades_verify_out_*.md` (research subagents; corrections applied as overrides in `scripts/z52b_upgrades_catalogue.py`). Items per upgrade in brackets.

1. **Ternary Goldbach** [`TG`, 7 items] — *before:* Hardy-Littlewood 1923 (all large odd n, conditional on GRH); Vinogradov 1937 (all sufficiently large odd n, unconditional, threshold astronomically large) — *after:* Helfgott 2013 (every odd n > 5) — *last step:* Explicit, sharpened minor-arc bounds (Vaughan identity, careful smoothing) + major arcs via rigorous numerical GRH verification for L-functions of conductor up to ~1.5e5 (Platt) + numerical verification of binary Goldbach to 4e18 / ternary to 8.875e30 (Helfgott-Platt) to close the gap. — *refs:* arXiv:1312.7748 (Helfgott, The ternary Goldbach problem); arXiv:1305.3062 (Helfgott-Platt, numerical verification up to 8.875e30)
2. **Binary Goldbach (stuck mirror)** [`BG`, 3 items] — *before:* Chudakov, van der Corput, Estermann 1938: almost all even n; Montgomery-Vaughan 1975: exceptional set <= N^{1-delta} — *after:* STILL OPEN for every n (verified to 4e18, Oliveira e Silva-Herzog-Pardi 2014) — *last step:* Not achieved: two prime variables give a main term ~ n/log^2 n but the minor arcs cannot be bounded pointwise in n (only in mean square). Lesson: 3 variables give enough room; 2 do not. — *refs:* Montgomery-Vaughan, Acta Arith. 27 (1975) 353-370; Oliveira e Silva-Herzog-Pardi, Math. Comp. 83 (2014)
3. **Chen's theorem** [`CHEN`, 4 items] — *before:* Renyi 1948: every large even n = p + P_K for some K; Brun — *after:* Chen 1966/1973: every large even n = p + P_2 — *last step:* Weighted (Kuhn/Richert) linear sieve with Bombieri-Vinogradov level 1/2 + Chen's switching principle (upper-bound the bad P_3 contribution by sieving the switched set). — *refs:* Chen Jingrun, Sci. Sinica 16 (1973) 157-176
4. **Schnirelmann constant (Goldbach-type every n with bounded number of primes)** [`SCHN`, 3 items] — *before:* Sieve upper bounds for r(n) = #{n = p + p'} + Cauchy-Schwarz (Schnirelmann): the set P+P (with 0, 1) has positive Schnirelmann density — *after:* Schnirelmann 1930: every n>1 is a sum of <= C primes; Ramare 1995: every even n is a sum of <= 6 primes; Tao 2014: every odd n>1 is a sum of <= 5 primes — *last step:* Schnirelmann's theorem: a set A containing 0 and 1 with positive Schnirelmann density is an additive basis of finite order; applied to {0,1} u (P+P) via an upper-bound sieve for r(n) and Cauchy-Schwarz. Explicit versions (Ramare) use explicit large sieve. — *refs:* Ramare, Ann. Scuola Norm. Sup. Pisa 22 (1995) 645-706; arXiv:1201.6656 (Tao, Every odd number greater than 1 is the sum of at most five primes)
5. **Goldbach-Linnik (two primes + K powers of 2)** [`GL`, 3 items] — *before:* Linnik 1951 (under GRH), 1953 unconditional: every large even n = p1 + p2 + 2^{v1}+...+2^{vK} — *after:* Heath-Brown-Puchta 2002: K=13; Pintz-Ruzsa: K=8 — *last step:* The binary Goldbach exceptional set is very small/structured and powers of 2 are spread out enough that n - (sum of K powers of 2) avoids it; analysis of exponential sums over powers of 2 on major arcs (measure of the set where |sum e(2^v alpha)| is large). — *refs:* Heath-Brown & Puchta, Asian J. Math 6 (2002) 535-565; Pintz-Ruzsa, Acta Arith. 109 (2003) 169-194 / doi:10.4064/aa109-3-1 (K=8)
6. **Waring's problem (Hilbert, g(k), G(k))** [`WAR`, 3 items] — *before:* Lagrange (k=2), Wieferich-Kempner g(3)=9; Hardy-Littlewood circle method: all sufficiently large n with s >= 2^k+1 kth powers — *after:* Hilbert 1909: every n is a sum of g(k) kth powers; Balasubramanian-Deshouillers-Dress 1986: g(4)=19; Chen 1964: g(5)=37 — *last step:* Hilbert: polynomial identities (Hilbert's identity expressing (x1^2+..+x5^2)^k as positive rational combination of (sum a_i x_i)^{2k}) + induction on k. g(4)=19: explicit circle method threshold (~10^367) + massive computation via 'descent' (greedy subtraction of biquadrates). — *refs:* Hilbert, Math. Ann. 67 (1909); Balasubramanian-Deshouillers-Dress, C. R. Acad. Sci. 303 (1986)
7. **Linnik: every large n is a sum of 7 cubes** [`LIN7`, 2 items] — *before:* Hardy-Littlewood: 8 cubes for large n (1920s); Davenport: almost all n are sums of 4 cubes — *after:* Linnik 1943: G(3) <= 7 (every sufficiently large n is a sum of 7 cubes); Watson 1951 simple proof — *last step:* Reduction of a cubic problem to representations by ternary quadratic forms (sums of 3 squares in an arithmetic progression) using an algebraic identity; then the theory of ternary forms / class numbers (every large n in the right classes is represented). — *refs:* Watson, J. London Math. Soc. 26 (1951) 153-156 (A simple proof that all large integers are sums of at most seven cubes)
8. **Vinogradov mean value theorem (main conjecture)** [`VMVT`, 2 items] — *before:* Vinogradov: near-optimal for s >> k^2 log k; Wooley efficient congruencing for k=3 and s in ranges — *after:* Bourgain-Demeter-Guth 2016 (all k), Wooley 2019 (nested efficient congruencing) — *last step:* l^2 decoupling for the moment curve: induction on scales with multilinear Kakeya / Brascamp-Lieb, bilinear-to-linear reduction. — *refs:* arXiv:1512.01565 (Bourgain-Demeter-Guth); arXiv:1708.01220 (Wooley, nested efficient congruencing)
9. **Hardy-Littlewood: n = p + x^2 + y^2** [`HLP`, 3 items] — *before:* Hooley 1957: every large n under GRH — *after:* Linnik 1960: every large n unconditional (asymptotic formula) — *last step:* Linnik's dispersion method + Bombieri-Vinogradov type input (later simplified via Barban/BV). A pointwise-in-n asymptotic for a sum over primes of a divisor-type function r_2(n-p). — *refs:* Linnik, The dispersion method in binary additive problems (AMS 1963)
10. **Lagrange four squares** [`LAG`, 4 items] — *before:* Fermat's claim; Euler's four-square identity (1748) reduces to primes; partial progress — *after:* Lagrange 1770: every n is a sum of 4 squares — *last step:* Euler's multiplicative identity + Lagrange's descent: for prime p find m p = sum of 4 squares with 1 <= m < p (pigeonhole on squares mod p), then strictly decrease m. — *refs:* standard (Hardy-Wright, Ch. XX)
11. **Fermat two squares (Zagier one-sentence proof)** [`TWOSQ`, 6 items] — *before:* Fermat claim; Euler 1749 proof by descent — *after:* every p = 1 (mod 4) is x^2 + y^2 (Euler); Heath-Brown 1984 / Zagier 1990 involution proofs — *last step:* Zagier: an explicit involution on S = {(x,y,z): x^2+4yz = p} has exactly one fixed point, so |S| is odd, so the involution (x,y,z)->(x,z,y) has a fixed point, giving x^2+(2y)^2=p. Parity replaces analysis. — *refs:* Zagier, Amer. Math. Monthly 97 (1990) 144; Heath-Brown, Invariant 1984
12. **Thue's lemma / pigeonhole for x^2 + y^2** [`THUE`, 3 items] — *before:* - — *after:* every p = 1 mod 4 (constructive pigeonhole) — *last step:* Thue 1902: for a coprime to p there are 0 < |x|,|y| < sqrt(p) with x = a y (mod p); apply with a^2 = -1. Pigeonhole in a box of side floor(sqrt p)+1. — *refs:* standard (Niven-Zuckerman)
13. **Gauss/Legendre three squares** [`THREESQ`, 4 items] — *before:* Legendre 1798 (proof relied on unproved Dirichlet primes in AP) — *after:* Gauss 1801: every n not of form 4^a(8b+7) is a sum of 3 squares — *last step:* Construct a positive ternary form of determinant 1 representing n (needs an auxiliary prime q in a progression with -n a QR mod q: Dirichlet), then use that every positive definite unimodular ternary integral lattice is isometric to Z^3 (one class in the genus of x^2+y^2+z^2). Local -> global through class number one. — *refs:* Serre, A Course in Arithmetic, Ch. IV; Ankeny, Proc. AMS 8 (1957) (sums of three squares via geometry of numbers)
14. **Fermat polygonal number theorem** [`CAUCHY`, 2 items] — *before:* Gauss Eureka 1796 (triangular), Lagrange (squares) — *after:* Cauchy 1813: every n is a sum of m m-gonal numbers — *last step:* Cauchy's lemma: for odd a, b with b^2 < 4a and 3a < b^2+2b+4 there are s,t,u,v >= 0 with a = s^2+t^2+u^2+v^2, b = s+t+u+v (simultaneous representation of norm and trace), via three squares. — *refs:* Nathanson, Proc. AMS 99 (1987) 22-24 (A short proof of Cauchy's polygonal number theorem)
15. **Tartakowsky / Kloosterman: quadratic forms in >= 4-5 variables** [`TART`, 4 items] — *before:* Hardy-Littlewood-Ramanujan sums of many squares; Siegel mass formula (genus average) — *after:* Tartakowsky 1929: positive form in >= 5 variables represents every sufficiently large n locally represented; Kloosterman 1926: 4 variables (with bounded divisibility by anisotropic primes) — *last step:* Bound on Fourier coefficients of the cuspidal part of the theta series (o(n^{k/2-1}) i.e. smaller than the Eisenstein (genus) main term); for 4 variables needs Kloosterman's nontrivial bound for Kloosterman sums (Kloosterman refinement of the circle method). — *refs:* Kloosterman, Acta Math. 49 (1926) 407-464; Hanke, survey 'Local densities and explicit bounds for representability by a quadratic form' Duke 124 (2004)
16. **Duke / Iwaniec / Duke-Schulze-Pillot: ternary forms** [`DUKE`, 5 items] — *before:* Linnik (ergodic method) under a splitting condition at a fixed prime; Siegel lower bound for class numbers h(-n) >> n^{1/2-eps} (ineffective) — *after:* Duke-Schulze-Pillot 1990: every sufficiently large n primitively represented by the spinor genus (and with bounded divisibility at anisotropic primes) is represented by a positive ternary form (ineffective) — *last step:* Iwaniec 1987: nontrivial bound for Fourier coefficients of half-integral weight cusp forms at squarefree n (n^{1/2-1/28+eps}) beating the Siegel lower bound for the Eisenstein part; Duke 1988 used it for equidistribution of lattice points on spheres / Heegner points. — *refs:* Iwaniec, Invent. Math. 87 (1987) 385-401; Duke, Invent. Math. 92 (1988) 73-90; Duke-Schulze-Pillot, Invent. Math. 99 (1990) 49-57
17. **Linnik's ergodic method (equidistribution on spheres) -> ELMV** [`LINNIK-ERG`, 3 items] — *before:* Linnik 1950s: equidistribution of integer points on spheres for n with (-n/p) = 1 for a fixed auxiliary prime p — *after:* Duke 1988 removed the fixed-split-prime condition (via Iwaniec's bound); the dynamical route (Einsiedler-Lindenstrauss-Michel-Venkatesh, 'Duke's theorem and Linnik's ergodic method' and Annals 2011 for cubic fields) still needs a split prime but recovers the result along such n. — *last step:* Either analytic (half-integral weight coefficient bound) or dynamical (maximal-entropy / measure rigidity for torus orbits, needs one fixed split prime). — *refs:* Einsiedler-Lindenstrauss-Michel-Venkatesh, Ann. Math. 173 (2011) (Distribution of periodic torus orbits and Duke's theorem for cubic fields) / Ergodic Theory Dynam. Systems (Duke's theorem and Linnik's ergodic method); Michel-Venkatesh ICM 2006 survey
18. **15 theorem / 290 theorem** [`FIFTEEN`, 3 items] — *before:* Ramanujan 1917 list of universal diagonal quaternary forms; Willerding — *after:* Conway-Schneeberger 1993 / Bhargava 2000 (15 theorem); Bhargava-Hanke 2005 (290 theorem) — *last step:* Escalation: any universal form contains an escalator lattice; finitely many escalators; for each 4-dim escalator, explicit bound on cusp-form coefficients (computed per form, Hanke) giving an explicit threshold + computer check of all n below it. — *refs:* Bhargava, The 15 theorem (Contemp. Math 272, 2000); Bhargava-Hanke, Universal quadratic forms and the 290-Theorem (preprint, 2005/2011; check publication status)
19. **Ramanujan's ternary form x^2+y^2+10z^2 (stuck at conditional)** [`RAMTERN`, 4 items] — *before:* Duke-Schulze-Pillot: all sufficiently large eligible squarefree n, ineffectively — *after:* Ono-Soundararajan 1997: under GRH, every eligible odd n except 3, 7, 21, 31, 33, 43, 67, 79, 87, 133, 217, 219, 223, 253, 307, 391, 679, 2719 is represented — *last step:* Waldspurger's formula (coefficient^2 ~ central L-value) + GRH-conditional explicit lower bound for the Eisenstein part vs. explicit upper bound for the cusp part + computation to 2*10^10. — *refs:* Ono-Soundararajan, Invent. Math. 130 (1997) 415-454
20. **Hasse principle for forms in many variables** [`HASSE`, 3 items] — *before:* Hasse-Minkowski (quadratic); Lewis/Davenport: cubic forms in many variables — *after:* Davenport 1959/1963: every cubic form in >= 16 variables has a nontrivial zero; Heath-Brown 1983: nonsingular cubic forms in >= 10 variables; Hooley 1988: Hasse principle for nonsingular cubic forms in 9 variables (given local solubility) — *last step:* Weyl differencing + geometry of numbers (Davenport's shrinking lemma) for minor arcs; Kloosterman refinement / van der Corput for the 10- and 9-variable cases; Birch 1962 for many variables via singular locus dimension. — *refs:* Davenport, Phil. Trans. A 251 (1959)/ Proc. Roy. Soc. A 272 (1963); Heath-Brown, Proc. London Math. Soc. 47 (1983) 225-257; Hooley, J. reine angew. Math. 386 (1988)
21. **Hypothesis H conditional -> unconditional for linear polynomials (fibration method)** [`BHS`, 2 items] — *before:* Colliot-Thelene-Sansuc / Serre / Swinnerton-Dyer: Hasse principle / BM for conic bundles etc. conditional on Schinzel's Hypothesis H — *after:* Harpaz-Skorobogatov-Wittenberg 2014: fibration method made unconditional for families whose degenerate fibres lie over Q-points (via Green-Tao-Ziegler); Browning-Matthiesen-Skorobogatov 2014: pencils of conics/quadrics with many (split-linear) degenerate fibres — *last step:* Green-Tao-Ziegler: asymptotics for linear equations in primes (finite complexity) replaces Hypothesis H for linear polynomials; Matthiesen's correlations of divisor-type functions. — *refs:* arXiv:1209.0207 (Browning-Matthiesen-Skorobogatov); arXiv:1304.3333 (Harpaz-Skorobogatov-Wittenberg, The Hardy-Littlewood conjecture and rational points)
22. **Linnik's least prime in AP** [`LINNIK`, 4 items] — *before:* Dirichlet 1837: infinitely many primes in every coprime class (ineffective size); GRH: P(q,a) << q^{2+eps} — *after:* Linnik 1944: P(q,a) << q^L for every q, a (Xylouris: L = 5) — *last step:* Three principles: log-free zero-density estimate, zero-free region, Deuring-Heilbronn repulsion of zeros by an exceptional zero (Siegel zero helps rather than hurts). — *refs:* Xylouris, Acta Arith. 150 (2011); Iwaniec-Kowalski, Analytic Number Theory, Ch. 18
23. **Dirichlet primes in AP** [`DIR`, 3 items] — *before:* Euler (q=1), special q via cyclotomic polynomials — *after:* Dirichlet 1837: every coprime class mod every q — *last step:* Non-vanishing L(1,chi) != 0 for all non-principal chi: complex chi by a positivity/product argument (prod_chi L(s,chi) has nonnegative coefficients), real chi via the class number formula (positivity of a class number). — *refs:* standard (Davenport, Multiplicative Number Theory, Ch. 1-4)
24. **Bertrand's postulate** [`BERT`, 6 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Chebyshev 1852; Erdos 1932 elementary: a prime in (n, 2n] for every n >= 1 — *last step:* Explicit bounds on the central binomial coefficient: primes in (2n/3, n] do not divide C(2n,n), and the remaining prime powers are too small to make up C(2n,n) >= 4^n/(2n+1) without a prime in (n,2n] -- an explicit threshold; small n checked by hand (e.g. the chain 2,3,5,7,13,23,43,83,163,317,631 each < twice the previous). — *refs:* Erdos, Acta Litt. Sci. Szeged 5 (1932) 194-198
25. **Primes in short intervals for every x** [`SHORT`, 5 items] — *before:* Selberg 1943: under RH, almost all intervals [x, x + f(x) log^2 x]; Huxley: almost all intervals of length x^{1/6+eps} — *after:* Hoheisel 1930: a prime in [x, x+x^{1-1/33000}] for large x; Baker-Harman-Pintz 2001: a prime in [x - x^{0.525}, x] for large x — *last step:* Zero-density estimates (Ingham, Huxley) + Harman's sieve: comparing a lower-bound sieve decomposition with a model, discarding only non-negative terms whose loss is numerically small. — *refs:* Baker-Harman-Pintz, Proc. London Math. Soc. 83 (2001) 532-562; Harman, Prime-Detecting Sieves (2007)
26. **Polya-Vinogradov -> Burgess (character sums / least non-residue)** [`BURGESS`, 3 items] — *before:* Linnik 1941: n(p) << p^eps for almost all p (large sieve invented for this); Vinogradov: n(p) << p^{1/(2 sqrt e)+eps} — *after:* Burgess 1957/62: nontrivial character sums of length p^{1/4+eps}; n(p) << p^{1/(4 sqrt e)+eps} for EVERY p; n(p) << p^eps for every p STILL OPEN — *last step:* Burgess amplification: shift trick (sum over n + ab), Holder, and Weil's RH for curves y^2 = f(x) to bound the complete high moment. — *refs:* Burgess, Proc. London Math. Soc. 12 (1962) 179-192; Linnik, Dokl. Akad. Nauk SSSR 30 (1941) (large sieve)
27. **Heilbronn: class number h(-d) -> infinity (Gauss conjecture)** [`HEIL`, 2 items] — *before:* Hecke: under GRH (no Siegel zeros) h(-d) -> infinity — *after:* Heilbronn 1934: unconditional h(-d) -> infinity; Siegel 1935 h >> d^{1/2-eps} (ineffective); Goldfeld-Gross-Zagier effective — *last step:* Case split: if GRH holds, Hecke's argument; if GRH fails, the false zero itself forces h(-d) large (Deuring-Heilbronn phenomenon). Effective: Goldfeld's criterion + Gross-Zagier L-function with order-3 zero. — *refs:* Heilbronn, Q. J. Math. 5 (1934) 150-160; Goldfeld, Bull. AMS 13 (1985) (Gauss's class number problem for imaginary quadratic fields)
28. **Siegel-zero conditional theorems (Heath-Brown twin primes)** [`SIEGELZ`, 1 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Heath-Brown 1983: if there are infinitely many exceptional (Siegel-zero) real characters, then there are infinitely many twin primes — *last step:* (heuristic summary) An exceptional real character chi with L(1,chi) tiny makes primes look like integers with chi(p) = -1, which is exploited through sieve/correlation estimates for the twin-prime problem. — *refs:* Heath-Brown, Proc. London Math. Soc. 47 (1983) 193-224 (Prime twins and Siegel zeros)
29. **Artin primitive root conjecture: all but two exceptions** [`ARTIN`, 3 items] — *before:* Hooley 1967 under GRH; Gupta-Murty 1984: one of 13 specific a is a primitive root for infinitely many p — *after:* Heath-Brown 1986: for all but at most two primes a, a is a primitive root mod infinitely many p — *last step:* Lower-bound sieve (Chen-type switching, Fouvry-Iwaniec level of distribution) produces many primes p for which (p-1)/2 has few prime factors, all large; for such p the index of the subgroup generated by a in F_p^* is tiny, and an independence/counting argument over any three multiplicatively independent candidates shows one of them is a primitive root for infinitely many such p (at most two exceptional primes). — *refs:* Heath-Brown, Q. J. Math. 37 (1986) 27-38 (Artin's conjecture for primitive roots)
30. **GPY -> Zhang/Maynard (conditional EH -> unconditional bounded gaps)** [`BV-EH`, 3 items] — *before:* Goldston-Pintz-Yildirim 2005: bounded gaps conditional on EH(theta > 1/2) — *after:* Zhang 2013: bounded gaps unconditionally; Maynard 2013 / Tao: unconditional, multidimensional sieve — *last step:* Zhang: equidistribution beyond 1/2 for smooth (y-friable) moduli with fixed residue (Weil/Deligne bounds for Kloosterman-type sums, Type I/II/III); Maynard: multi-dimensional Selberg weights so that level 1/2 suffices. — *refs:* Zhang, Ann. Math. 179 (2014) 1121-1174; arXiv:1311.4600 (Maynard, Small gaps between primes)
31. **Duffin-Schaeffer conjecture** [`DS`, 4 items] — *before:* Erdos-Vaaler, Pollington-Vaughan 1990 (conditions on psi; higher dims), Gallagher zero-one law — *after:* Koukoulopoulos-Maynard 2020: for every psi — *last step:* Quasi-independence on average of the approximation events, proved by controlling arithmetic overlaps (pairs with large gcd) with a structured iterative argument on 'GCD graphs' plus multiplicative-function / anatomy-of-integers estimates. — *refs:* arXiv:1907.04593 (Koukoulopoulos-Maynard, On the Duffin-Schaeffer conjecture)
32. **Friedlander-Iwaniec x^2+y^4 / Heath-Brown x^3+2y^3 (breaking parity)** [`FI`, 3 items] — *before:* Upper bound sieves only (parity barrier) — *after:* Friedlander-Iwaniec 1998: infinitely many (asymptotically) primes x^2+y^4; Heath-Brown 2001: x^3+2y^3 — *last step:* Asymptotic sieve for primes with bilinear (Type II) information extracted from the arithmetic of Z[i] (Friedlander-Iwaniec) resp. the cubic field Q(cbrt 2) (Heath-Brown, with his own Type II estimates). — *refs:* Friedlander-Iwaniec, Ann. Math. 148 (1998) 945-1040; Heath-Brown, Acta Math. 186 (2001) 1-84
33. **Titchmarsh divisor problem sum_{p<x} tau(p-1)** [`TITCH`, 3 items] — *before:* Titchmarsh 1930 under GRH — *after:* Linnik 1961 unconditional (dispersion); Fouvry 1985 and Bombieri-Friedlander-Iwaniec 1986 (Acta Math. 156, 99-131 via primes in AP to large moduli) gave improved error terms — *last step:* Bombieri-Vinogradov theorem (or dispersion) to handle divisors up to sqrt x and a switching d <-> (p-1)/d for the large divisors. — *refs:* Fouvry, J. reine angew. Math. 357 (1985); Bombieri-Friedlander-Iwaniec, Acta Math. 156 (1986) 203-251 (Primes in arithmetic progressions to large moduli)
34. **Green-Tao-Ziegler: linear equations in primes** [`GTZ`, 3 items] — *before:* Hardy-Littlewood conjecture (conditional); Vinogradov for 3-term ternary — *after:* Green-Tao 2008 (arbitrarily long APs), Green-Tao 2010 + Green-Tao-Ziegler 2012 (all finite complexity linear systems) — *last step:* Transference principle (pseudorandom majorant), inverse theorem for Gowers norms, Mobius orthogonal to nilsequences. — *refs:* arXiv:math/0404188 (Green-Tao, primes contain arbitrarily long APs); arXiv:math/0606088 (Green-Tao, Linear equations in primes, Ann. Math. 171 (2010) 1753-1850); arXiv:1009.3998 (Green-Tao-Ziegler, inverse theorem for U^{s+1})
35. **Schinzel Hypothesis H for 100% of polynomials (stuck mirror)** [`SKSO`, 3 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Skorobogatov-Sofos 2022: Schinzel's Hypothesis holds on average over families of polynomials (with applications to rational points); no single nonlinear polynomial known — *last step:* Averaging over the family of polynomials (large sieve over coefficient space); does not give any individual polynomial. — *refs:* arXiv:2005.02998 (Skorobogatov-Sofos, Schinzel Hypothesis on average and rational points)
36. **Fermat's Last Theorem** [`FLT`, 4 items] — *before:* Kummer (regular primes); Faltings 1983 (finitely many solutions per n); Heath-Brown/Granville 1985: FLT for almost all exponents; Adleman-Heath-Brown-Fouvry 1985 first case for infinitely many p — *after:* Wiles 1995 (with Taylor-Wiles 1995 patching): every n >= 3 — *last step:* Frey curve + Ribet's level lowering + modularity of semistable elliptic curves (R = T, Taylor-Wiles patching, 3-5 switch). A hypothetical solution would yield a weight-2 newform of level 2; none exists. — *refs:* Wiles, Ann. Math. 141 (1995) 443-551; Heath-Brown, Bull. LMS 17 (1985) 15-16 (Fermat's last theorem for 'almost all' exponents)
37. **Catalan's conjecture** [`CAT`, 3 items] — *before:* Tijdeman 1976: finitely many solutions, effectively bounded (Baker); computations narrowed exponents — *after:* Mihailescu 2002: only 3^2 - 2^3 = 1 — *last step:* Algebraic, via cyclotomic fields: Mihailescu's double-Wieferich criterion (p^{q-1} = 1 mod q^2 and q^{p-1} = 1 mod p^2), annihilators of cyclotomic class groups (Stickelberger; Thaine's theorem for the real part, per Bilu's Bourbaki expose 909) and cyclotomic units; the final proof avoids heavy computation and Baker bounds. — *refs:* Mihailescu, J. reine angew. Math. 572 (2004) 167-195; Bilu, Seminaire Bourbaki 909 (2002)
38. **Bang-Zsigmondy / Bilu-Hanrot-Voutier primitive divisors** [`ZSIG`, 3 items] — *before:* Bang 1886 (a=2), Zsigmondy 1892; Lucas/Lehmer: Schinzel, Stewart (all n beyond an effective bound) — *after:* Bilu-Hanrot-Voutier 2001: every Lucas/Lehmer number u_n with n > 30 has a primitive divisor — *last step:* Zsigmondy: size of cyclotomic value Phi_n(a,b) vs. its possible non-primitive prime factors (only the largest prime of n, to the first power). BHV: Baker-type linear forms in logs (Voutier) give an explicit n bound, then solving Thue equations / enormous computation for the remaining n. — *refs:* Bilu-Hanrot-Voutier, J. reine angew. Math. 539 (2001) 75-122
39. **Thue-Siegel-Roth** [`ROTH`, 1 items] — *before:* Liouville: exponent d; Thue: d/2+1; Siegel; Dyson — *after:* Roth 1955: exponent 2+eps for every algebraic number — *last step:* Auxiliary polynomial in many variables vanishing to high index at (alpha,...,alpha) + Roth's lemma (non-vanishing at rational points with rapidly growing heights). — *refs:* Roth, Mathematika 2 (1955) 1-20
40. **Baker's theorem -> class number one (Heegner/Baker/Stark)** [`BAKER`, 2 items] — *before:* Heilbronn-Linfoot: at most 10 imaginary quadratic fields of class number 1 — *after:* Heegner 1952, Baker 1966, Stark 1967: exactly 9 — *last step:* Baker: lower bounds for linear forms in logarithms make the hypothetical 10th discriminant effectively bounded; Heegner/Stark: modular functions / CM values (Weber) give a Diophantine equation with few solutions. — *refs:* Stark, Michigan Math. J. 14 (1967); Baker, Mathematika 13 (1966)
41. **Pell equation: every non-square D** [`PELL`, 3 items] — *before:* Brahmagupta/Bhaskara chakravala for many D — *after:* Lagrange 1768: x^2 - D y^2 = 1 has a nontrivial solution for every non-square D — *last step:* Dirichlet's proof: infinitely many (x,y) with |x^2 - D y^2| < 1 + 2 sqrt D (Dirichlet approximation); pigeonhole: infinitely many share the same norm k and the same residues (x,y) mod |k|; for two such, (x1 x2 - D y1 y2)/k and (x1 y2 - x2 y1)/k are integers with norm 1, nontrivial. (Equivalently: periodicity of the continued fraction of sqrt D, Lagrange.) Fails exactly at squares. — *refs:* standard (Niven-Zuckerman-Montgomery Ch. 7)
42. **Congruent numbers (stuck mirror)** [`CONG`, 2 items] — *before:* Heegner/Monsky: primes 5,7 mod 8; Tian 2014: infinitely many congruent n with any given number of prime factors in each class 5,6,7 mod 8 — *after:* Smith 2017-: distribution of 2^infty-Selmer groups in quadratic twist families (consequences for congruent numbers on density level, under stated hypotheses); every n = 5,6,7 mod 8 congruent STILL OPEN (follows from BSD) — *last step:* Tian: induction on number of prime factors via Gross-Zagier + Waldspurger and an explicit Heegner point non-triviality criterion. — *refs:* Tian, Camb. J. Math. 2 (2014) 117-161; arXiv:1702.02325 (Smith, 2^infinity-Selmer groups...)
43. **Gross-Zagier-Kolyvagin (BSD in rank <= 1)** [`GZK`, 2 items] — *before:* BSD conjectural; Coates-Wiles for CM curves — *after:* Gross-Zagier 1986 + Kolyvagin 1988: every elliptic curve over Q (modular, now all) with analytic rank 0 or 1 has algebraic rank = analytic rank and finite Sha — *last step:* Heegner points (explicit points from CM theory) + Euler system bounding Selmer groups. — *refs:* Gross-Zagier, Invent. Math. 84 (1986); Kolyvagin 1988
44. **Sums of three cubes (stuck mirror)** [`THREECUBES`, 1 items] — *before:* Heath-Brown: conjecture density of representations; computations — *after:* Booker 2019 found 33, Booker-Sutherland 2021 found 42 and a third representation of 3; the conjecture that every k != +-4 mod 9 is a sum of three cubes is STILL OPEN — *last step:* Computation only (Elkies-type lattice/ Booker's algorithm); no every-k mechanism. — *refs:* arXiv:1903.04284 (Booker, Cracking the problem with 33); Booker-Sutherland, PNAS 118 (2021)
45. **Ramanujan-Petersson conjecture (holomorphic)** [`RAM`, 2 items] — *before:* Rankin-Selberg: on average; Hecke bound tau(n) << n^{6}; Rankin n^{29/5} — *after:* Deligne 1974: |tau(p)| <= 2 p^{11/2} for every p — *last step:* Realising tau(p) as trace of Frobenius on etale cohomology of Kuga-Sato varieties (Eichler-Shimura-Deligne) + Weil conjectures (purity; tensor power trick). — *refs:* Deligne, Publ. IHES 43 (1974) 273-307
46. **RH for curves (Weil; Stepanov-Bombieri)** [`WEIL`, 4 items] — *before:* Hasse 1933: elliptic curves; Davenport-Hasse special curves — *after:* Weil 1948: every curve; Stepanov 1969/Bombieri 1973 elementary proofs — *last step:* Stepanov-Bombieri: an auxiliary polynomial vanishing to high order at the F_q-points yields an UPPER bound N <= q + O(g sqrt q); in standard treatments the matching LOWER bound comes from applying upper bounds to complementary objects (twists/characters, or a Galois cover whose point counts are related) rather than directly. — *refs:* Bombieri, Seminaire Bourbaki 430 (1973); Stepanov 1969
47. **Quantum unique ergodicity (arithmetic)** [`QUE`, 5 items] — *before:* Shnirelman-Zelditch-Colin de Verdiere: quantum ergodicity (density-one subsequence of eigenfunctions equidistributes) — *after:* Lindenstrauss 2006 + Soundararajan 2010: every Hecke-Maass eigenform on SL2(Z)\H equidistributes — *last step:* Lindenstrauss: Hecke recurrence -> positive entropy on almost every ergodic component (Bourgain-Lindenstrauss) -> measure rigidity => limit is c*Haar; Soundararajan: no escape of mass (c=1) via multiplicativity of Hecke eigenvalues (lambda(p)^2 = lambda(p^2)+1 prevents both small) and a sieve-type mean value argument. — *refs:* Lindenstrauss, Ann. Math. 163 (2006) 165-219; arXiv:0901.4060 (Soundararajan, QUE for SL2(Z)\H)
48. **Holomorphic QUE (Holowinsky-Soundararajan)** [`HQUE`, 4 items] — *before:* Holowinsky: QUE for all but a sparse set of forms (sieve bound on shifted convolutions); Soundararajan: weak subconvexity works except when Hecke eigenvalues are unusually distributed — *after:* Holowinsky-Soundararajan 2010: every holomorphic Hecke eigenform (weight -> infinity) — *last step:* Holowinsky (sieve bounds for shifted convolution sums) and Soundararajan (weak subconvexity) each give QUE except for forms with atypical Hecke-eigenvalue statistics; their exceptional conditions are incompatible, so combining the two yields every form (informal summary of the joint paper). — *refs:* arXiv:0809.1636 (Holowinsky-Soundararajan, Mass equidistribution for Hecke eigenforms)
49. **Subconvexity / amplification (Friedlander-Iwaniec, Duke-Friedlander-Iwaniec, Michel-Venkatesh)** [`SUBCONV`, 4 items] — *before:* Lindelof on average (moments of families) — *after:* Duke-Friedlander-Iwaniec 1993: subconvexity for L(1/2, f x chi) in the conductor aspect of chi (f fixed); Michel-Venkatesh 2010: subconvexity for GL2 automorphic L-functions in all aspects (uniformly over number fields) — *last step:* Amplification: embed the individual L-function in a family, weight the family's moment by a short Dirichlet polynomial (amplifier) that is large at the target member, and bound the amplified moment. — *refs:* Duke-Friedlander-Iwaniec, Invent. Math. 112 (1993); arXiv:0903.3591 (Michel-Venkatesh, The subconvexity problem for GL2)
50. **Sato-Tate (conditional on functoriality -> unconditional)** [`SATO`, 3 items] — *before:* Serre: Sato-Tate follows if all symmetric power L-functions are automorphic — *after:* Clozel-Harris-Shepherd-Barron-Taylor 2008 / Barnet-Lamb-Geraghty-Harris-Taylor 2011: every non-CM elliptic curve over Q (and over totally real fields); every non-CM holomorphic newform of weight >= 2 — *last step:* Potential automorphy: symmetric powers become automorphic over some (varying) totally real extension; Brauer's induction theorem shows that is enough for meromorphic continuation and non-vanishing on Re s = 1. — *refs:* Barnet-Lamb-Geraghty-Harris-Taylor, Publ. RIMS 47 (2011) 29-98 (A family of Calabi-Yau varieties and potential automorphy II); Barnet-Lamb-Geraghty-Harris-Taylor, JAMS 24 (2011) 411-469? (Sato-Tate for modular forms; check)
51. **Serre's modularity conjecture** [`SERREMOD`, 2 items] — *before:* Known for small conductors/weights; potential modularity (Taylor) — *after:* Khare-Wintenberger 2009: every odd irreducible 2-dim mod-p Galois rep of Gal(Qbar/Q) is modular — *last step:* Induction on conductor and weight: 'killing ramification' via compatible systems and modularity lifting, reducing to base cases (Tate/Serre: no level-1 irreducible reps in characteristic 2, 3). — *refs:* Khare-Wintenberger, Invent. Math. 178 (2009) 485-504 and 505-586
52. **Bourgain-Gamburd expansion / Helfgott growth** [`BGEXP`, 4 items] — *before:* Selberg 3/16: congruence quotients of arithmetic lattices are expanders (every q); thin groups: no spectral theory — *after:* Bourgain-Gamburd 2008: for a finite symmetric S in SL2(Z) generating a Zariski-dense subgroup, the Cayley graphs of SL2(F_p) w.r.t. S mod p form an expander family as p ranges over primes (large p); later squarefree q (Bourgain-Gamburd-Sarnak, Bourgain-Varju) — *last step:* Helfgott's product theorem in SL2(F_p) (|AAA| >= |A|^{1+delta} unless A ~ whole group) + Bourgain-Gamburd flattening (non-concentration on subgroups via escape from subvarieties) + Sarnak-Xue quasirandomness (large minimal representation dimension of SL2(F_p)). — *refs:* Bourgain-Gamburd, Ann. Math. 167 (2008) 625-642; Helfgott, Ann. Math. 167 (2008) 601-623
53. **Bourgain-Katz-Tao sum-product in F_p** [`SUMPROD`, 2 items] — *before:* Erdos-Szemeredi over R (1983); finite fields open — *after:* Bourgain-Katz-Tao 2004: for every prime p, every A in F_p with p^delta < |A| < p^{1-delta} has max(|A+A|,|AA|) >= |A|^{1+eps} — *last step:* Combinatorial: Balog-Szemeredi-Gowers + Plunnecke + a structure argument that a set almost closed under + and * must be a subfield; F_p has no subfields. — *refs:* arXiv:math/0301343 (Bourgain-Katz-Tao)
54. **Markoff triples mod p (strong approximation)** [`MARKOFF`, 5 items] — *before:* Bourgain-Gamburd-Sarnak 2016: Markoff graphs mod p connected for all p outside a set of density zero (and with bounded number of exceptions in any... ), all p for which p^2-1 is not too smooth — *after:* W. Chen 2024 (Annals 199): every connected component of the Markoff mod p graph has size divisible by p; combined with BGS (any component is either small and absent or the giant one), connectivity for all but finitely many primes — *last step:* Chen: Markoff triples mod p parametrise SL2(F_p)-structures on punctured elliptic curves; the degree of each relevant component of a map between Hurwitz-type moduli spaces is divisible by p (algebraic-geometric/topological divisibility via Nielsen equivalence), not analytic counting. — *refs:* arXiv:2011.12940 (Chen, Nonabelian level structures, Nielsen equivalence, and Markoff triples, Ann. Math. 2024); arXiv:1607.01530 (Bourgain-Gamburd-Sarnak, Markoff triples and strong approximation)
55. **Apollonian local-global (FAILED upgrade)** [`APOLLO`, 4 items] — *before:* Bourgain-Kontorovich 2014: almost every admissible integer is a curvature; Bourgain-Fuchs positive density — *after:* Haag-Kertzer-Rickards-Stange (arXiv 2023): the local-global conjecture is FALSE (quadratic and quartic reciprocity obstructions remove whole families such as squares times constants in some packings) — *last step:* Reciprocity obstruction: a Kronecker/quartic residue symbol constant on the orbit; the almost-all theorem cannot be upgraded; the corrected conjecture excludes the reciprocity families. — *refs:* arXiv:2307.02749 (Haag-Kertzer-Rickards-Stange, The local-global conjecture for Apollonian circle packings is false)
56. **Zaremba's conjecture (bounded partial quotients)** [`ZAREMBA`, 6 items] — *before:* Niederreiter 1986: every power of 2, 3, 5 (A=3/4); Bourgain-Kontorovich 2014: density one for A=50; Huang 2015: A=5; Shkredov-Moshchevitin modular forms — *after:* every q with A=5 STILL OPEN; Moshchevitin-Shkredov (Pacific J. Math 309 (2020)): for every prime p there is q = 0 mod p, q = O(p^30), and a coprime to q with a/q having bounded partial quotients (modular Zaremba) — *last step:* Bourgain-Kontorovich: thin-orbit circle method, spectral gap of congruence towers for the Hausdorff-dimension-close-to-1 Cantor semigroup. Modular version: sum-product / growth in SL2(F_p) (Helfgott/Bourgain-Gamburd) for the semigroup reduced mod p. — *refs:* arXiv:1107.3776 (Bourgain-Kontorovich, On Zaremba's conjecture); arXiv:1911.07487 (Moshchevitin-Shkredov, On a modular form of Zaremba's conjecture); Niederreiter, Monatsh. Math. 101 (1986)
57. **Oppenheim conjecture** [`OPP`, 3 items] — *before:* Davenport-Heilbronn / Birch-Davenport-Ridout: >= 21 then >= 5 variables (circle method) — *after:* Margulis 1987: every indefinite irrational nondegenerate quadratic form in >= 3 variables has values dense at 0 — *last step:* Homogeneous dynamics: Margulis's 1987 proof establishes the needed case of Raghunathan's orbit-closure conjecture for the action of SO(2,1)-type (unipotent-generated) subgroups on SL3(R)/SL3(Z) by topological unipotent-dynamics arguments; Ratner's general theorems came later. — *refs:* Margulis, C. R. Acad. Sci. 304 (1987); Margulis, Oppenheim conjecture survey (Fields Medallists' lectures 1997)
58. **Ratner / every-orbit equidistribution (Dani-Smillie horocycles)** [`RATNER`, 3 items] — *before:* Birkhoff ergodic theorem: almost every orbit equidistributes — *after:* Dani-Smillie 1984: every non-closed horocycle orbit on finite-volume hyperbolic surfaces equidistributes; Ratner 1991: every unipotent orbit closure is homogeneous — *last step:* Measure rigidity / unique ergodicity of horocycle flow (Furstenberg 1973 compact case) + non-divergence (Dani-Margulis) to prevent escape to the cusp. — *refs:* Dani-Smillie, Duke Math. J. 51 (1984) 185-194; Ratner, Ann. Math. 134 (1991)
59. **Weyl equidistribution for every irrational** [`WEYL`, 2 items] — *before:* Bohl/Sierpinski/Weyl 1909-10 (n alpha); metric (a.e.) results for general sequences (Koksma) — *after:* Weyl 1916: (polynomial with an irrational non-constant coefficient)(n) is equidistributed mod 1 for EVERY such polynomial — *last step:* Weyl criterion + van der Corput / Weyl differencing (reduce degree until linear). — *refs:* Weyl, Math. Ann. 77 (1916) 313-352
60. **Furstenberg x2 x3 (topological every-orbit vs measure)** [`FURST`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Furstenberg 1967: every infinite closed x2,x3-invariant subset of the circle is the whole circle (every irrational orbit is dense); measure version (Rudolph positive entropy) still open — *last step:* Non-lacunary multiplicative semigroup {2^a 3^b} has gaps ratio -> 1 + structure of closed invariant sets. — *refs:* Furstenberg, Math. Systems Theory 1 (1967) 1-49
61. **Large values / Kolmogorov counterexample (a.e. cannot be upgraded)** [`SNIRBOUND`, 3 items] — *before:* Carleson 1966: Fourier series of L^2 functions converge a.e. — *after:* Kolmogorov 1926: an L^1 function whose Fourier series diverges everywhere (so 'a.e. -> every' fails in L^1); Kahane-Katznelson: every null set is a divergence set for some continuous function — *last step:* Counterexample constructions show where almost-all is sharp; relevant as a test: is ES's exceptional set structurally like a Kahane-Katznelson null set (any null set can be bad) or not? — *refs:* Kolmogorov, Fund. Math. 4 (1923)/ C. R. 1926 (everywhere divergent L^1 Fourier series); Kahane-Katznelson, Acta Math. 1966 (doi:10.1007/BF02392438)
62. **Sendov's conjecture** [`SENDOV`, 4 items] — *before:* Proved for degree <= 8 (Brown-Xiang), various special cases — *after:* Tao 2020: true for all sufficiently large degree n — *last step:* Compactness/contradiction: a sequence of counterexamples with n -> infinity yields limiting zero/critical-point measures; detailed potential-theoretic and analytic estimates (logarithmic potential, Newton-type identities) rule them out for large n. — *refs:* arXiv:2012.04125 (Tao, Sendov's conjecture for sufficiently high degree polynomials)
63. **Erdos discrepancy problem** [`EDP`, 4 items] — *before:* Konev-Lisitsa 2014: discrepancy > 2 by SAT (C=2); results for completely multiplicative sequences — *after:* Tao 2015: every +-1 sequence has unbounded discrepancy — *last step:* Fourier reduction to (approximately) completely multiplicative functions + logarithmically averaged Elliott-type correlation estimate (from Matomaki-Radziwill-Tao) + entropy decrement argument. — *refs:* arXiv:1509.05363 (Tao, The Erdos discrepancy problem)
64. **Sensitivity conjecture** [`SENS`, 2 items] — *before:* Polynomial relations among many measures known except sensitivity — *after:* Huang 2019: every induced subgraph on 2^{n-1}+1 vertices of the n-cube has max degree >= sqrt(n) — *last step:* A signed adjacency matrix A_n of the cube with A_n^2 = nI (eigenvalues +-sqrt n, each multiplicity 2^{n-1}) + Cauchy interlacing. — *refs:* arXiv:1907.00847 (Huang, Induced subgraphs of hypercubes and a proof of the Sensitivity Conjecture)
65. **Finite-field Kakeya** [`KAKEYA-FF`, 1 items] — *before:* Wolff bound, additive-combinatorics bounds — *after:* Dvir 2008: every Kakeya set in F_q^n has size >= c_n q^n — *last step:* Polynomial method: if the Kakeya set K were small, a nonzero polynomial of degree < q vanishing on K exists; restricting to each line in K shows the top-degree homogeneous part vanishes at every direction, hence identically (degree < q), contradiction. — *refs:* arXiv:0803.2336 (Dvir, On the size of Kakeya sets in finite fields)
66. **Kakeya in R^3** [`KAKEYA3`, 3 items] — *before:* Wolff 5/2, Katz-Laba-Tao 5/2+eps, Katz-Zahl, Wang-Zahl sticky case — *after:* Wang-Zahl 2025: every Kakeya set in R^3 has Hausdorff and Minkowski dimension 3 — *last step:* Multi-scale induction on 'stickiness'/convex-set volume estimates, Furstenberg set estimates (Ren-Wang), reduction of the general case to sticky-like structure. — *refs:* arXiv:2502.17655 (Wang-Zahl, Volume estimates for unions of convex sets, and the Kakeya set conjecture in three dimensions)
67. **Cap sets** [`CAPSET`, 2 items] — *before:* Meshulam 1995: r_3(F_3^n) << 3^n/n (density increment); Bateman-Katz — *after:* Croot-Lev-Pach 2016 / Ellenberg-Gijswijt 2017: |A| <= 2.756^n for every cap set — *last step:* Polynomial method / slice rank of the tensor 1[x+y+z=0] restricted to A. — *refs:* arXiv:1605.01506 (Croot-Lev-Pach, Progression-free sets in Z_4^n are exponentially small); arXiv:1605.09223 (Ellenberg-Gijswijt)
68. **Erdos-Ginzburg-Ziv** [`EGZ`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* EGZ 1961: every 2n-1 integers contain n with sum = 0 mod n — *last step:* Reduction to primes: EGZ(n) for n = ab follows from EGZ(a) and EGZ(b) (iterated extraction); for primes, Cauchy-Davenport or Chevalley-Warning (the latter: sum x_i^{p-1} = 0, sum a_i x_i^{p-1} = 0 in 2p-1 variables). (Original 1961 proof was combinatorial.) — *refs:* Erdos-Ginzburg-Ziv, Bull. Res. Council Israel 10F (1961)
69. **Kemnitz conjecture** [`KEMNITZ`, 2 items] — *before:* Known for many n; Ronyai 4n-2 — *after:* Reiher 2007: every 4n-3 lattice points in Z^2 contain n with integral centroid — *last step:* Chevalley-Warning applied to suitable auxiliary polynomial systems mod p combined with a double-counting of subsequences with prescribed sizes and sums (Reiher's short proof), then multiplicativity in n. — *refs:* Reiher, Ramanujan J. 13 (2007) 333-337
70. **Davenport constant (Olson)** [`DAV`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Olson 1969: D(G) = 1 + sum (n_i - 1) for every finite abelian p-group (and rank 2) — *last step:* Group ring F_p[G]: product of (1 - x^{g_i}) is nonzero iff ... ; nilpotency of the augmentation ideal. — *refs:* Olson, J. Number Theory 1 (1969) 8-10
71. **Cauchy-Davenport / Erdos-Heilbronn** [`CAUDAV`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Cauchy 1813 / Davenport 1935: |A+B| >= min(p, |A|+|B|-1) for every p; Dias da Silva-Hamidoune 1994: restricted sums (Erdos-Heilbronn) — *last step:* Alon-Nathanson-Ruzsa polynomial method / Combinatorial Nullstellensatz (Alon 1999). — *refs:* Alon, Combin. Probab. Comput. 8 (1999) 7-29 (Combinatorial Nullstellensatz)
72. **Chevalley-Warning** [`CHEVW`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Chevalley 1935/Warning: every system with sum of degrees < n variables over F_q has #solutions = 0 mod p (so a nontrivial zero if one trivial) — *last step:* Counting solutions mod p via 1 - f^{q-1} indicator; sum of monomials over F_q^n vanishes in low degree. — *refs:* standard
73. **Gowers quasirandom groups / Nikolov-Pyber product covering** [`GOWERS`, 3 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Gowers 2008; Nikolov-Pyber 2011: if |A||B||C| > |G|^3/m (m = minimal degree of nontrivial representation) then ABC = G — *last step:* Spectral (representation-theoretic) bound shows AB has almost all of G; then |AB| + |C| > |G| forces g C^{-1} to meet AB for every g: pigeonhole turns 'almost all' into 'all'. — *refs:* arXiv:0710.3877 (Gowers, Quasirandom groups); arXiv:math/0703343 (Nikolov-Pyber, Product decompositions of quasirandom groups and a Jordan type theorem)
74. **Ore conjecture / Waring problem for finite simple groups** [`ORE`, 3 items] — *before:* Many families; asymptotic results (Liebeck-Shalev: every element is product of two commutators for large G) — *after:* Liebeck-O'Brien-Shalev-Tiep 2010: every element of every finite non-abelian simple group is a commutator; Larsen-Shalev-Tiep 2011: for every nontrivial word w, w(G)^2 = G for all sufficiently large finite simple G — *last step:* Classification-based case analysis: character-theoretic estimates (Frobenius formula N(g) = |G| sum chi(g)/chi(1) with character ratio bounds) for large rank, plus case-by-case and computational arguments for small rank / small q. — *refs:* arXiv:0904.4146? (Liebeck-O'Brien-Shalev-Tiep, The Ore conjecture, J. Eur. Math. Soc. 12 (2010)); Larsen-Shalev-Tiep, Ann. Math. 174 (2011)
75. **Existence of designs (Keevash; Glock-Kuhn-Lo-Osthus)** [`DESIGNS`, 2 items] — *before:* Rodl 1985 nibble: approximate Steiner systems (almost all pairs covered); Wilson 1975: graph decompositions for large admissible n — *after:* Keevash 2014: Steiner systems / designs exist for every admissible n >= n_0; Glock-Kuhn-Lo-Osthus 2016 iterative absorption — *last step:* Randomised algebraic construction / iterative absorption: build in advance a small 'absorber' that can fix any leftover from an approximate (nibble) decomposition. — *refs:* arXiv:1401.3665 (Keevash, The existence of designs); arXiv:1611.06827 (Glock-Kuhn-Lo-Osthus, The existence of designs via iterative absorption)
76. **Wilson's existence theory (PBD closure)** [`WILSON`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Wilson 1972-75: for a fixed block-size set K, a PBD(v,K) exists for all sufficiently large v satisfying the necessary divisibility (admissibility) conditions — *last step:* PBD-closure: B(K) is closed under a recursive 'filling in' operation; an eventually periodic closed set contains all large admissible n once it has generators in each residue class. — *refs:* Wilson, J. Combin. Theory A 13 (1972) 220-245, 246-273; 18 (1975)
77. **Erdos-Faber-Lovasz / Ringel / Ryser-Brualdi-Stein (all large n via absorption)** [`EFL`, 2 items] — *before:* Asymptotic versions (Kahn 1992 for EFL) — *after:* Kang-Kelly-Kuhn-Methuku-Osthus 2021: EFL for all large n; Montgomery-Pokrovskiy-Sudakov 2021: Ringel for large n; Montgomery 2023: Ryser-Brualdi-Stein transversal of size n-1 for large n — *last step:* Broad shared toolkit: random nibble (approximate decomposition) + absorbers fixing the leftover + extremal/non-extremal structural case analysis (details differ per problem). — *refs:* arXiv:2101.04698 (Kang-Kelly-Kuhn-Methuku-Osthus); arXiv:2001.02665 (Montgomery-Pokrovskiy-Sudakov); arXiv:2310.19779 (Montgomery, Ryser-Brualdi-Stein)
78. **Four colour theorem** [`FOURCOL`, 3 items] — *before:* Heawood 1890 five colours; Kempe (flawed); Heesch discharging — *after:* Appel-Haken 1976; Robertson-Sanders-Seymour-Thomas 1997 — *last step:* Unavoidable set of reducible configurations found by discharging, each reducibility checked by computer: every planar graph contains one of 633 configurations, none can be in a minimal counterexample. — *refs:* Robertson-Sanders-Seymour-Thomas, J. Combin. Theory B 70 (1997) 2-44
79. **Kahn-Kalai expectation threshold (Park-Pham)** [`PARK`, 2 items] — *before:* Talagrand fractional version (Frankston-Kahn-Narayanan-Park 2019) — *after:* Park-Pham 2022: p_c <= K q(F) log l for every monotone property — *last step:* Spread/minimal-cover counting argument (from sunflower work of Alweiss-Lovett-Wu-Zhang). — *refs:* arXiv:2203.17207 (Park-Pham, A proof of the Kahn-Kalai conjecture)
80. **Union-closed sets (Gilmer)** [`UNIONCL`, 2 items] — *before:* Constant 1/log2 n fractions only — *after:* Gilmer 2022: every union-closed family has an element in at least a constant fraction (0.01) of sets; subsequently improved to (3-sqrt 5)/2 ~ 0.38 by several groups — *last step:* Entropy: for independent A, B from the family, H(A u B) >= H(A) bound unless some element is frequent. — *refs:* arXiv:2211.09055 (Gilmer, A constant lower bound for the union-closed sets conjecture)
81. **Lovasz Local Lemma / Janson (existence from limited dependence)** [`LLL`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Erdos-Lovasz 1975 (symmetric LLL: if each bad event has probability <= p, depends on <= d others, and e p (d+1) <= 1, then with positive probability none occurs); Janson's inequality P(no event) <= exp(-mu + Delta/2) for monotone events built from independent bits — *last step:* Dependency-graph induction; Janson: P(no event) <= exp(-mu + Delta/2) for monotone events from independent bits. — *refs:* Erdos-Lovasz 1975; Janson-Luczak-Rucinski, Random Graphs Ch. 2
82. **Polynomial Freiman-Ruzsa** [`PFR`, 2 items] — *before:* Sanders quasi-polynomial bounds — *after:* Gowers-Green-Manners-Tao 2023: polynomial bounds in F_2^n (every set with small doubling) — *last step:* Entropic Ruzsa distance and an entropy-decrement iteration. — *refs:* arXiv:2311.05762 (Gowers-Green-Manners-Tao, On a conjecture of Marton)
83. **Erdos-Graham monochromatic unit fractions (Croot)** [`CROOT`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Croot 2003: every finite colouring of the integers > 1 has a monochromatic set with sum 1/n_i = 1 — *last step:* Fourier analysis on smooth numbers + combinatorial lemma producing many representations with structured denominators. — *refs:* arXiv:math/0311421 (Croot, On a coloring conjecture about unit fractions, Ann. Math. 157 (2003))
84. **Bloom: unit fractions in dense sets** [`BLOOM`, 2 items] — *before:* Croot (colouring version) — *after:* Bloom 2021: every set of positive upper density contains a finite S with sum 1/n = 1; Liu-Sawhney 2024: any A in [1,N] with sum_{n in A} 1/n >= (log N)^{4/5+o(1)} contains such S — *last step:* Counting solutions with a main term from arithmetic structure (integers with many small prime factors / restricted anatomy) plus exponential sum estimates for the error (details: see paper). — *refs:* arXiv:2112.03726 (Bloom, On a density conjecture about unit fractions); arXiv:2404.07113 (Liu-Sawhney, On further questions regarding unit fractions)
85. **Graham: every n > 77 is a sum of distinct integers with reciprocals summing to 1** [`GRAHAM77`, 3 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Graham 1963: every integer > 77 is partitionable into distinct parts with sum 1/a_i = 1 — *last step:* Closure maps: if n is representable then so are 2n+2 and 2n+9 (use 1 = 1/2 + sum 1/(2 a_i), etc.) + finite check for 78..333. — *refs:* Graham, A theorem on partitions, J. Austral. Math. Soc. 3 (1963) 435-441 (77-theorem); see also Graham, Pacific J. Math. 10 (1960) 1221-1224 / OEIS A051882 (subagent dating conflict: check)
86. **Fibonacci-Sylvester greedy algorithm** [`FIBSYL`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* every positive rational is a finite sum of distinct unit fractions (Fibonacci 1202, Sylvester 1880) — *last step:* Greedy: numerator strictly decreases. (Open for odd greedy algorithm termination.) — *refs:* standard
87. **Breusch/Stewart: odd denominators** [`BREUSCH`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Breusch 1954 / Stewart 1954: every rational with odd denominator is a finite sum of distinct unit fractions with odd denominators — *last step:* Constructive splitting using 1/n = 1/(n+1) + 1/(n(n+1)) variants restricted to odd; a covering argument by residue. — *refs:* Breusch, A special case of Egyptian fractions, Amer. Math. Monthly 61 (1954) 200-201; Stewart, Sums of distinct divisors, Amer. J. Math 76 (1954)
88. **Erdos minimum modulus problem for covering systems** [`HOUGH`, 3 items] — *before:* Erdos 1950 question; Filaseta-Ford-Konyagin-Pomerance-Yu 2007 (sum 1/m constraints) — *after:* Hough 2015: every distinct covering system has a modulus <= 10^16; Balister-Bollobas-Morris-Sahasrabudhe-Tiba 2022 distortion method (<= 616000); Hough-Nielsen 2019: every covering has a modulus divisible by 2 or 3 — *last step:* Lovasz-Local-Lemma-type sieve on the uncovered set with a 'distortion' of the measure at each prime stage so that the uncovered density stays positive. — *refs:* arXiv:1307.0874 (Hough, Solution of the minimum modulus problem for covering systems, Ann. Math. 181 (2015)); arXiv:2211.01417 (Balister-Bollobas-Morris-Sahasrabudhe-Tiba, distortion method, bound 616000); arXiv:1703.02133 (Hough-Nielsen, Covering systems with restricted divisibility)
89. **Goldbach's other conjecture (FAILED: cautionary)** [`GOLDOTHER`, 2 items] — *before:* Goldbach 1752: every odd composite = p + 2k^2; checked by Goldbach/Euler for small n — *after:* FALSE: Stern 1856 found 5777 and 5993 — *last step:* (Assessment, not from a source) The representation count r(n) = #{(p,k): n = p + 2k^2} has mean of order sqrt(n)/log n, still small near n ~ 6000; sporadic failures in the small range are therefore unsurprising. Lesson: check left-tail statistics, not just the mean. — *refs:* OEIS A060003 (Stern primes / counterexamples)
90. **Partition congruences for every prime modulus** [`ONO`, 3 items] — *before:* Ramanujan congruences for 5, 7, 11; Atkin for some others — *after:* Ono 2000: for every prime m >= 5 there are infinitely many arithmetic progressions An+B with p(An+B) = 0 mod m; Ahlgren-Ono 2001 for all m coprime to 6 — *last step:* Modular forms mod m: half-integral weight forms, Shimura lift and Serre's results on Hecke operators acting as scalars mod m for a positive proportion of primes (Chebotarev for Galois reps); see the paper for the full argument. — *refs:* Ono, Ann. Math. 151 (2000) 293-307 (Distribution of the partition function modulo m)
91. **Kneser conjecture (topological method)** [`KNESER`, 3 items] — *before:* Special cases — *after:* Lovasz 1978: chromatic number of Kneser graph KG(n,k) is n-2k+2 for every n, k — *last step:* Borsuk-Ulam theorem: existence forced by topology (antipodal maps), no counting at all. — *refs:* Lovasz, J. Combin. Theory A 25 (1978) 319-324
92. **Thomassen: every planar graph is 5-choosable (strengthened induction)** [`THOM`, 2 items] — *before:* Voigt: not 4-choosable; Erdos-Rubin-Taylor conjecture — *after:* Thomassen 1994: every planar graph is 5-choosable — *last step:* Prove a much stronger statement (outer face precoloured on two adjacent vertices, outer vertices with lists of size 3) that is amenable to induction. — *refs:* Thomassen, J. Combin. Theory B 62 (1994) 180-181
93. **Sun's 1-3-5 conjecture (refined Lagrange)** [`SUN135`, 2 items] — *before:* Z.-W. Sun 2017 conjecture: every n = x^2+y^2+z^2+w^2 with x+3y+5z a square; numerics — *after:* Machiavelo-Tsopanidis 2021: proved for every n — *last step:* Arithmetic representation argument on sums of four squares with a linear side condition (Machiavelo-Tsopanidis; method details: check the paper). — *refs:* arXiv:2003.02592 (Machiavelo-Tsopanidis, Zhi-Wei Sun's 1-3-5 conjecture and variations)
94. **Primes x^2 + n y^2 (class field theory)** [`CFT`, 3 items] — *before:* Fermat/Euler/Lagrange/Gauss for small n by reciprocity and genus theory — *after:* Every n: for odd primes p not dividing n, p = x^2 + n y^2 iff p splits completely in the ring class field of Z[sqrt(-n)] (Weber, Takagi) — *last step:* Class field theory: a Chebotarev/Artin-symbol condition replaces genus theory when the class group is not 2-torsion. — *refs:* Cox, Primes of the form x^2 + ny^2 (1989)
95. **Function-field analogues (Goldbach over F_q[T], twin primes for large q)** [`FFGOLD`, 3 items] — *before:* Conditional / heuristic analogues — *after:* Effinger-Hayes 1991: ternary Goldbach over F_q[T] for all large degree; Sawin-Shusterman 2022 (separate work): twin prime and Chowla analogues over F_q[T] for suitable fixed q — *last step:* RH for curves (Weil/Deligne) makes major/minor arcs explicit; Sawin-Shusterman: etale cohomology of Hurwitz-type spaces / vanishing cycles to get cancellation uniform in degree. — *refs:* Effinger-Hayes, Additive Number Theory of Polynomials over a Finite Field (1991); arXiv:1808.04001 (Sawin-Shusterman, On the Chowla and twin primes conjectures over F_q[T])
96. **Matomaki-Radziwill: multiplicative functions in almost all short intervals (stuck mirror)** [`MR`, 2 items] — *before:* Classical zero-density results give cancellation of mu/lambda in EVERY interval [x, x+h] only for h >= x^{7/12+eps} (Huxley-type) — *after:* Matomaki-Radziwill 2016: cancellation of bounded multiplicative functions (e.g. Liouville) in almost all intervals of any length h -> infinity; the every-interval statement for much shorter h remains open — *last step:* Pointwise-to-average via Parseval + Halasz on Dirichlet polynomials; the upgrade to every interval is blocked by inability to control one interval (same shape as ES). — *refs:* arXiv:1501.04585 (Matomaki-Radziwill, Multiplicative functions in short intervals)
97. **Negative Pell / Stevenhagen (density, stuck mirror)** [`NEGPELL`, 2 items] — *before:* Fouvry-Kluners bounds — *after:* Koymans-Pagano 2022: Stevenhagen's conjecture on density of D with x^2 - D y^2 = -1 solvable — *last step:* Smith's method for 2^k-Selmer / class-group distributions combined with new reflection principles for 4-ranks/8-ranks; only densities, no individual criterion. — *refs:* arXiv:2201.13424 (Koymans-Pagano, On Stevenhagen's conjecture)
98. **Shafarevich: every solvable group is a Galois group over Q** [`SHAF`, 3 items] — *before:* Scholz-Reichardt: every p-group (p odd) — *after:* Shafarevich 1954: every finite solvable group — *last step:* Induction on solvable length via embedding problems with prescribed local behaviour (choose auxiliary primes carefully; Neukirch's later correction for 2-groups). — *refs:* Neukirch-Schmidt-Wingberg, Cohomology of Number Fields, Ch. IX
99. **Zeros on the critical line: infinitely many -> positive proportion (stuck gradient)** [`HARDYZ`, 2 items] — *before:* Hardy 1914: infinitely many zeros on Re s = 1/2 — *after:* Selberg 1942: positive proportion; Levinson 1974: >= 1/3; Conrey 1989: >= 2/5 (100% still open) — *last step:* Mollifiers: multiply by a short Dirichlet polynomial approximating 1/zeta to damp large values before a mean-square argument. — *refs:* Conrey, J. reine angew. Math. 399 (1989) 1-26
100. **Siegel's theorem (average class numbers -> individual lower bound)** [`SIEGEL`, 3 items] — *before:* Gauss/Lipschitz/Mertens: mean values of h(-d); Landau: L(1,chi) >> 1/sqrt q with at most one exception — *after:* Siegel 1935: L(1,chi) >> q^{-eps} (h(-d) >> d^{1/2-eps}) for every real chi (ineffective) — *last step:* Two-character trick: the Dedekind zeta function of the biquadratic field, zeta L(s,chi1) L(s,chi2) L(s,chi1 chi2), has nonnegative coefficients; a lower bound for it near s=1 combined with a hypothetical exceptional chi1 forces every other chi2 to have L(1,chi2) not too small. — *refs:* Siegel, Acta Arith. 1 (1935) 83-86; Iwaniec-Kowalski, Analytic Number Theory, Thm 5.28
101. **PRIMES is in P (randomized / conditional -> deterministic unconditional)** [`AKS`, 3 items] — *before:* Miller 1976: deterministic polynomial primality test assuming ERH; Solovay-Strassen / Miller-Rabin randomized — *after:* Agrawal-Kayal-Saxena 2002 (Ann. Math. 160 (2004)): deterministic polynomial-time primality test for every n, unconditionally — *last step:* Polynomial congruence (X+a)^n = X^n + a mod (X^r - 1, n) for a small range of a, plus a counting argument: if n is not a prime power, a certain group of polynomials would have to be both large (many distinct products of (X+a)) and small (bounded by n^{sqrt t}), contradiction. — *refs:* Agrawal-Kayal-Saxena, Ann. Math. 160 (2004) 781-793
102. **Spencer's six standard deviations (random -> every set system)** [`SPENCER`, 2 items] — *before:* Random colouring: discrepancy O(sqrt(n log n)) for n sets on n points — *after:* Spencer 1985: every system of n sets on n points has discrepancy <= 6 sqrt n — *last step:* Partial colouring lemma via entropy/pigeonhole (Beck): exponentially many colourings fall into few discrepancy buckets, two collide, and their half-difference is a partial colouring with small discrepancy; iterate. — *refs:* Spencer, Trans. AMS 289 (1985) 679-706 (Six standard deviations suffice)
103. **Euler's 36 officers: orthogonal Latin squares for every n != 2, 6** [`MOLS`, 2 items] — *before:* Euler 1782 conjecture (no pair for n = 2 mod 4); MacNeish product construction for some n — *after:* Bose-Shrikhande-Parker 1960: a pair of orthogonal Latin squares exists for every n except 2 and 6 (Euler's conjecture false) — *last step:* Recursive constructions (pairwise balanced designs, products) reducing every n to a finite set of base cases plus direct constructions. — *refs:* Bose-Shrikhande-Parker, Canad. J. Math. 12 (1960) 189-203
104. **Heawood map colouring (Ringel-Youngs)** [`RINGEL`, 3 items] — *before:* Heawood 1890 upper bound; many genera done individually — *after:* Ringel-Youngs 1968: chromatic number of every orientable surface of genus g >= 1 equals the Heawood number — *last step:* Case split into 12 residue classes of n mod 12; each class needs its own family of 'current graph' constructions; some classes required much harder ad hoc work. — *refs:* Ringel-Youngs, PNAS 60 (1968) 438-445
105. **Erdos: odd numbers not of the form p + 2^k (covering-system obstruction; cautionary)** [`ERDCOV`, 2 items] — *before:* Romanov 1934: numbers p + 2^k have positive lower density; naive heuristics suggest few exceptions — *after:* Erdos 1950: an infinite arithmetic progression of odd numbers not of the form p + 2^k (via the covering system 0(2),0(3),1(4),3(8),7(12),23(24)); Sierpinski numbers 78557*2^n+1 likewise — *last step:* A finite covering system makes every candidate p = n - 2^k divisible by one of a few small primes: an obstruction that is invisible to density heuristics and not a Brauer-Manin obstruction. — *refs:* Erdos, Summa Brasil. Math. 2 (1950) 113-123 (On integers of the form 2^k + p and some related problems)
106. **Skolem-Mahler-Lech** [`SML`, 3 items] — *before:* Skolem 1934 (rational), Mahler 1935 (algebraic) — *after:* Lech 1953: the zero set of every linear recurrence over a field of characteristic 0 is a finite union of arithmetic progressions plus a finite set — *last step:* p-adic analytic interpolation: on each residue class mod a suitable M, n -> a_n is a p-adic analytic function, whose zeros are isolated unless it vanishes identically. — *refs:* Lech, Ark. Mat. 2 (1953) 417-421
107. **Sphere packing in dimensions 8 and 24** [`VIAZ`, 3 items] — *before:* Cohn-Elkies 2003 linear programming bound (numerically near-sharp in dims 8, 24) — *after:* Viazovska 2016 (dim 8), Cohn-Kumar-Miller-Radchenko-Viazovska 2016 (dim 24): E8 and Leech lattice packings are optimal — *last step:* Construction of an exact 'magic' auxiliary function (dual certificate for the LP bound) from quasimodular forms / weakly holomorphic modular forms via a Laplace-transform-type integral, with sign conditions verified analytically. — *refs:* arXiv:1603.04246 (Viazovska, The sphere packing problem in dimension 8); arXiv:1603.06518 (Cohn-Kumar-Miller-Radchenko-Viazovska, dim 24)
108. **Littlewood's conjecture (stuck mirror; measure rigidity)** [`LITTLE`, 2 items] — *before:* - — *after:* Einsiedler-Katok-Lindenstrauss 2006: the exceptional set has Hausdorff dimension 0; full conjecture STILL OPEN — *last step:* Measure rigidity for higher-rank diagonal actions on SL3(R)/SL3(Z) with positive entropy. — *refs:* Einsiedler-Katok-Lindenstrauss, Ann. Math. 164 (2006) 513-560
109. **Rademacher exact formula (asymptotic -> exact for every n)** [`RADEM`, 3 items] — *before:* Hardy-Ramanujan 1918: asymptotic series for p(n) (divergent if continued) — *after:* Rademacher 1937: a convergent exact series for p(n) for every n; extended to coefficients of weakly holomorphic modular forms of non-positive weight, and (Bringmann-Manschot 2013) to certain mixed mock modular generating functions of sheaf invariants on P^2 — *last step:* Circle method with Farey dissection taken to infinite order + exact modular transformation of the generating function; the Kloosterman-sum-weighted Bessel series converges and the first term dominates with an explicitly bounded tail (Lehmer). — *refs:* Rademacher, Proc. London Math. Soc. (2) 43 (1937) 241-254; arXiv:1006.0915 (Bringmann-Manschot, From sheaves on P^2 to a generalization of the Rademacher expansion, Amer. J. Math. 135 (2013) 1039-1065)
110. **Poincare conjecture (high dimensions -> 4 -> 3)** [`POINC`, 2 items] — *before:* Smale 1961: dimension >= 5 (h-cobordism); Freedman 1982: dimension 4 — *after:* Perelman 2002-03: dimension 3 (every simply connected closed 3-manifold is S^3) — *last step:* Ricci flow with surgery (Hamilton's program) + Perelman's monotone quantities (entropy, reduced volume) + finite extinction time for simply connected manifolds (separate paper). — *refs:* arXiv:math/0211159 (Perelman, The entropy formula for the Ricci flow); arXiv:math/0303109 (Perelman, Ricci flow with surgery on three-manifolds); arXiv:math/0307245 (Perelman, Finite extinction time)
111. **Bieberbach conjecture (case-by-case -> every n)** [`BIEB`, 2 items] — *before:* Bieberbach 1916 (n=2), Loewner 1923 (n=3), Garabedian-Schiffer (n=4), Pederson/Ozawa (n=6), Pederson-Schiffer (n=5); Milin conjecture formulated — *after:* de Branges 1985: |a_n| <= n for every n and every normalised univalent function — *last step:* Loewner differential equation + a family of weight functions whose required positivity reduces to the Askey-Gasper inequality for sums of Jacobi polynomials (a special-function positivity identity valid for all n). — *refs:* de Branges, Acta Math. 154 (1985) 137-152
112. **Collatz: almost all orbits attain almost bounded values (stuck mirror)** [`COLLATZ`, 3 items] — *before:* Terras/Everett 1976-77: almost all n have a finite stopping time; Korec — *after:* Tao 2019: almost all (log density) orbits attain values below f(n) for any f -> infinity; every orbit STILL OPEN — *last step:* Approximate the Syracuse map's 3-adic statistics by a random walk and prove a fine-scale mixing (Fourier decay) estimate for the Syracuse random variable; the step to every n is not available. — *refs:* arXiv:1909.03562 (Tao, Almost all orbits of the Collatz map attain almost bounded values)
113. **Ax-Kochen: Artin's C2 conjecture for all large p (via ultraproducts)** [`AXKOCHEN`, 2 items] — *before:* Lang: F_q((t)) is C2 (every form of degree d in > d^2 variables has a nontrivial zero); Artin's conjecture that Q_p is C2 — *after:* Ax-Kochen 1965: for each d, forms of degree d in > d^2 variables have nontrivial p-adic zeros for all p > p_0(d) (and Terjanian 1966: false for some small p) — *last step:* Model-theoretic transfer: non-principal ultraproducts of Q_p and of F_p((t)) are elementarily equivalent, so first-order statements true for F_p((t)) for almost all p hold for Q_p for almost all p. — *refs:* Ax-Kochen, Amer. J. Math. 87 (1965) 605-630; Terjanian, C. R. Acad. Sci. Paris Ser. A-B 262 (1966) A612
114. **Sylvester-Schur (prime factors of products of consecutive integers / AP terms)** [`SYLV`, 2 items] — *before:* Bertrand (k = n case); Sylvester 1892 — *after:* Sylvester 1892 / Schur 1929 / Erdos 1934: for n >= k, the product (n+1)...(n+k) has a prime factor > k; Shorey-Tijdeman: analogues for products of terms of arithmetic progressions — *last step:* Compare the size of the product with the maximal contribution of primes <= k (Legendre's formula / binomial-coefficient bounds). — *refs:* Erdos, J. London Math. Soc. 9 (1934) 282-288 (A theorem of Sylvester and Schur)
115. **Erdos-Selfridge: a product of consecutive integers is never a perfect power** [`ERDSELF`, 1 items] — *before:* Many special cases (Liouville, Rigge, Erdos 1939 for large k) — *after:* Erdos-Selfridge 1975: the product of two or more consecutive positive integers is never a perfect power — *last step:* Write each factor as a_i * (power); the a_i are distinct and small, and an elementary combinatorial counting of the a_i (plus Sylvester-type prime factors) gives a contradiction. — *refs:* Erdos-Selfridge, Illinois J. Math. 19 (1975) 292-301
116. **Undirected connectivity in logspace (randomized -> deterministic for every graph)** [`REINGOLD`, 2 items] — *before:* Aleliunas-Karp-Lipton-Lovasz-Rackoff 1979: randomized logspace via random walks — *after:* Reingold 2005/2008: deterministic logspace for every undirected graph (SL = L) — *last step:* Iteratively power a regularised input graph and reduce the degree with zig-zag/replacement products: connectivity is preserved while the spectral gap is amplified, until short walks can be enumerated deterministically. — *refs:* Reingold, J. ACM 55 (2008) Art. 17 (Undirected connectivity in log-space)
